# Development Notes

## Codex usage experiment

PacePrompt records Codex model-usage counters for each Codex-assisted commit. This is an engineering-process experiment modelled on WeeklyHealthReport's development notes. The figures measure repeated model processing across a task, not source-code size, unique conversation text, developer productivity or human effort.

The API-equivalent value is a comparison estimate only. This work runs through a ChatGPT subscription and is not billed as API usage. Token pricing does not include any separately priced API tools or external services unless a row explicitly says otherwise.

## Recording protocol

1. Assign the change a stable ID in the form `PP-YYYYMMDD-NN`.
2. Record the task's exact cumulative usage snapshot before implementation begins.
3. Immediately before the commit, record another snapshot after code, tests, review and this note are complete.
4. Subtract the start snapshot from the end snapshot. If no valid start snapshot exists, label the result as a task aggregate rather than a per-commit delta.
5. Add one row to the table below and include `PacePrompt-Change: <change-id>` as a commit-message trailer. The trailer maps the row to the resulting commit without attempting to place a commit's own hash inside itself.
6. Record the exact model, official pricing URL and pricing date used. Recheck pricing whenever the model or published rate changes.

Record these counters separately:

- total input tokens;
- cached input tokens, which are included within total input;
- cache-write input tokens, when reported;
- output tokens, including any reasoning tokens already counted within output;
- total tokens.

Do not reconstruct missing counters from source size, elapsed time or subscription usage. Mark missing measurements as `Unmeasured`.

## API-equivalent calculation

For GPT-5.6 Sol, the official promotional rates checked on 2 September 2026 and rechecked on 3 September 2026 are $4.00 per million uncached input tokens, $0.40 per million cached input tokens and $20.00 per million output tokens. Cache writes are billed at 1.25 times the uncached-input rate. Prompts above the model's published long-context threshold require request-level calculation rather than applying a single aggregate rate.

For a phase with no cache-write or long-context adjustment:

```text
uncached input = total input - cached input
API-equivalent =
    (uncached input / 1,000,000 × input rate)
  + (cached input / 1,000,000 × cached-input rate)
  + (output / 1,000,000 × output rate)
```

Pricing authority: [OpenAI GPT-5.6 Sol model documentation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.6-sol).

For Claude Fable 5.1 (`claude-fable-5-1`), the official rates checked on 4 September 2026 are $10.00 per million uncached input tokens, $0.25 per million cache-read input tokens, $12.50 per million five-minute-TTL cache-write input tokens (1.25 times the input rate), $20.00 per million one-hour-TTL cache-write input tokens (2 times the input rate) and $50.00 per million output tokens. Claude Code sessions in this repository use the one-hour cache TTL, and the session JSONL reports cache writes per TTL, so each cache write is priced at its TTL-specific rate. Claude Code counters come from the session file under `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session-id>.jsonl`: assistant records are deduplicated by `message.id` because one API response is written as several lines that repeat the same usage; `input_tokens` is the uncached input; total input is uncached input plus cache reads plus cache writes; thinking tokens are already a subset of output.

For a Claude Code phase with TTL-specific cache writes:

```text
API-equivalent =
    (uncached input / 1,000,000 × input rate)
  + (cache-read input / 1,000,000 × cache-read rate)
  + (five-minute cache-write input / 1,000,000 × five-minute write rate)
  + (one-hour cache-write input / 1,000,000 × one-hour write rate)
  + (output / 1,000,000 × output rate)
```

Pricing authority for Claude models: [Anthropic pricing documentation](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/pricing).

`PP-20260925-02` adds a narrow injected display-wake capability at the application composition root. PacePrompt now disables the iOS idle timer only after the accepted Begin workout transition, keeps it disabled through every nonterminal exercise stage, and releases it immediately for finished, failed and interrupted states as well as coordinator reset. The execution binding notifies its owner after every reduced event so disconnection and other asynchronous terminal paths release the request without waiting for a view refresh. Settings explains both this automatic behaviour and the separate, user-controlled Guided Access procedure for physically restricting the iPhone to PacePrompt; the app does not claim to enter Guided Access itself. Exhaustive policy tests cover every exercise stage, a real synthetic disconnect proves the immediate interruption path, and the UI test covers the user-facing guidance. No FTMS command, Bluetooth capability, reconnection, treadmill-control, HealthKit, provider, persistence or background-mode behaviour changed.

Focused validation passed 21 `ProductionWorkoutExecutionBindingTests` and the updated Settings UI test. The first complete gate encountered a crashed CoreSimulator service and terminated 22 unrelated UI cases; after restarting that service, the first genuine failure passed in isolation and a fresh complete `scripts/validate_local.sh` run passed on the final executable and test inputs. The replacement gate passed 219 HostEval tests with 20 expected skips, all 387 production Simulator tests (338 unit and 49 UI) with no failures or skips, the unsigned Release Simulator build, static analysis, resource and safety checks, all 16 evaluation-target tests, 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`. Evidence is retained at `/private/tmp/paceprompt-screen-awake-final-retry`. This is Simulator evidence only: no physical iPhone, FR30z treadmill, Bluetooth link or Guided Access session was operated.

`PP-20260919-02` implements issue #131's bounded same-process continuity policy for an already-connected, already-running workout. Inactive, background and lock transitions retain the current peripheral, link, connection epoch, held-control state, FR30z profile, subscriptions and frozen speed/inclination limits without initiating a new connection or restoration path. Interactive controls remain foreground-only. While backgrounded, only a fresh matching telemetry notification may advance one already-planned transition; the binding revalidates the connection, epoch, profile, control, limits, command deadlines and current machine state before dispatch, and preserves ATT response, FTMS result and observed-state confirmation as separate requirements. Timer progress is derived from accepted telemetry evidence rather than wall-clock catch-up. A telemetry gap longer than the two-second freshness window enters checking, excludes the unobserved interval and requires foreground reconciliation before ordinary progress can continue. Process termination, Bluetooth loss, peripheral disconnect, control loss, stale or malformed evidence, profile change and frozen-limit change remain fail-closed. A bounded local checkpoint records only enough identity and accepted execution evidence to classify an interrupted history entry after a process end; it cannot recreate a connection, control lease, programme or pending effect, and it is removed after classification. The explicit app property list declares only `bluetooth-central`; source and built-product checks reject restoration hooks, disallowed background modes and any FTMS Start, Stop or Pause route. Documentation records the product, timing, persistence and evidence boundaries. Independent exact-head review found that a successful background acknowledgement could leave an initial, manual or restoration target sequence idle after foregrounding; foreground reconciliation now resumes only the already-authorised sequence after fresh evidence and the existing guards, with deterministic coverage for all three paths. Follow-up review found that the final background acknowledgement could defer creation of its observation deadline until foreground reconciliation, allowing a later matching sample to receive a fresh deadline. Final acknowledgement now installs that deadline independently of authority to submit another write, and deterministic coverage proves that a matching sample at the expired boundary fails closed without target submission. The replacement complete gate at `/private/tmp/paceprompt-131-deadline-fix-final-gate` passed 336 unit tests, 49 UI tests, Debug and Release simulator builds with exact built-property-list verification, static analysis, 14 evaluation tests, 24 accounting-helper tests, host checks and `git diff --check`. This is software and simulator evidence only: no signed iPhone lifecycle observation, Bluetooth treadmill connection, physical FR30z capability, transition acknowledgement or machine-state confirmation is claimed by the gate.

`PP-20260919-01` adds operator runbooks for the complete internal TestFlight process established by issue #120. The setup guide covers App Store Connect team keys, Keychain CSR and Apple Distribution certificate creation, PKCS#12 export, the explicit App Store provisioning profile, local inspection, the protected GitHub environment, secrets, variables and tag rules. The release guide covers unique version/build selection, metadata sources, focused and complete validation, exact-head review and merge, protected tag creation, environment approval, processing and separate tester-availability verification. The recovery guide requires read-only Apple inspection before any retry and distinguishes failures before upload, ambiguous uploads, Apple processing failures and tester-access problems. The index fixes the production app identity and evidence boundaries, while the README and implementation reference now point to the operator procedures. All examples use placeholders; a known-identifier and private-key-material scan passed. The documented App Store Connect group-discovery endpoints were exercised read-only against the existing app without printing identifiers. Twenty synthetic no-upload release tests, release-configuration verification, `actionlint`, local Markdown-link checks and `git diff --check` passed. This documentation-only change leaves every executable, test, build, workflow and validation-script input byte-identical to current `main`, so the complete simulator gate remains valid under the repository validation policy and was not duplicated. No credential was created or changed, no tag or upload was attempted, and no App Store, TestFlight, iPhone, HealthKit or treadmill acceptance claim is made.

`PP-20260913-03` repairs the target-observation failure exposed by issue #62's second supervised physical attempt. Request Control, the initial 0.50 km/h and 0% targets, the planned 0.60 km/h transition and a manual 0.70 km/h speed override each received separate ATT acceptance and matching FTMS success. A later Treadmill Data report decoded the protocol's raw 70 speed field as binary `Double` `0.7000000000000001`; the previous string-based `Double`-to-`Decimal` bridge preserved that representation tail, so the exact reducer correctly refused to equate it with the canonical 0.70 km/h target and failed closed after the observation deadline. The operator separately observed 0.70 km/h, physically stopped the treadmill and confirmed it stopped; the app explicitly disconnected once. Those operator statements are not treadmill telemetry evidence, the failed attempt does not satisfy issue #62, and its sanitised evidence is retained in `docs/validation/fr30z-workout-proof-target-observation-abort-2026-09-13.md` without a peripheral identifier or raw packets.

The repair centralises conversion of integer-scaled FTMS values at the protocol-to-domain boundary: speed becomes an exact two-decimal `Decimal` and inclination an exact one-decimal `Decimal`. Both live workout telemetry and advertised capability ranges use the same conversion. Regression coverage includes the observed 0.70 km/h manual-override flow, 19.99 km/h and 0.30% representation tails, capability minima, maxima and increments, and non-finite rejection. The reducer retains exact comparisons; the Control Point encoder retains its bounded representation tolerance and off-grid rejection; and freshness, observation deadlines, command ordering, per-write authority checks and fail-closed behaviour are unchanged. FTMS Start, Stop and Pause remain absent, with no retry, reconnect, control reacquisition or further physical session in this repair phase.

The focused parser, production-binding, treadmill-presentation, reducer and Control Point codec suites passed, followed by an unsigned proof-flagged Release generic-iOS build. The frozen executable and test inputs then passed one final `scripts/validate_local.sh` gate: host and corpus checks; all 327 app, unit and UI tests; Release simulator build; static analysis; 14 evaluation tests; 24 accounting-helper tests; safety scans; coverage export; project validation; and `git diff --check`. Final complete-gate evidence is retained at `/private/tmp/paceprompt-issue62-decimal-final`. Simulator, build and test results are not physical-device, Bluetooth, protocol-acknowledgement, treadmill-state or motion evidence.

`PP-20260913-02` removes the stationary-Treadmill-Data deadlock from issue #62's initial workout ordering. Exact-profile readiness and a confirmed Control Point indication subscription may expose arming without a received Treadmill Data packet. Begin workout now creates only the local attempt and waits for physical Start with control not held and no procedure effect. A stationary report, its expiry, or packet absence keeps that command-free pre-control wait unknown. The first fresh, complete, current-epoch non-zero Treadmill Data report permits exactly one Request Control procedure; matching ATT acceptance and FTMS success permit the initial speed-then-inclination sequence only while movement evidence remains fresh, otherwise another fresh non-zero report is required. Current movement before Begin and malformed or contradictory evidence still block, while all per-transmission authority, foreground, epoch, profile, capability, procedure, frozen-attempt, ceiling and freshness checks remain. FTMS Start, Stop and Pause remain absent, with no automatic retry, reconnect, control reacquisition or belt command.

`PP-20260913-01` adds a proof-only issue #62 app composition behind `PACEPROMPT_ISSUE62_PROOF`; the ordinary Release composition remains locked and passive. The proof host fixes one conservative walking plan at 60 seconds at 0.50 km/h and 0%, 180 seconds at 0.60 km/h and 0%, then 60 seconds at 0.50 km/h and 0%, with ceilings of 0.70 km/h, 1% and a 0.10 km/h planned-step speed change. Every command edge retains the exact FR30z profile, foreground, current connection epoch, complete fresh telemetry, frozen attempt and ceiling checks, and one-procedure-at-a-time transport. The only representable procedures remain Request Control, Set Target Speed and Set Target Inclination; physical-console Start and Stop remain authoritative, and FTMS Start, Stop and Pause remain absent. There is no automatic retry, reconnect or resume.

The repair replaces the persistent build-lifetime one-shot with an in-memory authorization scoped to one explicit connection sequence. A completed, interrupted, failed or disconnected sequence invalidates that authorization and control assumptions; a later explicit reconnection can be independently authorised without rebuilding or reinstalling. Within an authorised running sequence, the operator may make repeated reducer-valid, in-range speed and inclination adjustments, return to the current plan target, and record repeated physical Stop and resume observations. Those markers do not command the belt. The sanitised report separates sequences, lifecycle and execution states, records whether any executable control phase was entered, and does not include a peripheral identifier or raw packet data. Disconnecting before Begin stays in setup rather than presenting a misleading completed workout view.

The first supervised physical attempt established the operator-confirmed exact profile but did not receive Treadmill Data while the belt was stationary. Begin was never selected, no executable control phase was entered, and no Request Control or target procedure was transmitted. The old proof authority was nevertheless consumed by connection authorisation and the app was explicitly disconnected; that usability defect is the reason for this reusable-session repair. The attempt is not issue #62 acceptance evidence, and no replacement physical session is included in this change.

`PP-20260912-06` implements only issue #61's target-only production binding from exact `origin/main` `e7b686ad83078a7254b481fe742d647ba4614cbb`. The app-owned CoreBluetooth edge exposes one write-with-response operation for the discovered FTMS Control Point and reports indication subscription, ATT delivery and indication callbacks without selecting commands, retrying or reconnecting. The production adapter can map only Request Control, Set Target Speed and Set Target Inclination reducer effects; the codec and transport no longer represent FTMS Start, Stop or Pause. Immediately before every write, the adapter rechecks the separately injected proof-session authority and frozen session identity, connection epoch, foreground state, exact FR30z capability/profile evidence, current reducer procedure, one-procedure transport state, control permission, frozen attempt and ceilings, and complete fresh telemetry. Ordinary production construction uses an authority that always returns no active proof session, with no setting, launch argument or persisted unlock route. App inactivity, connection loss, profile drift, control loss or authority expiry suppresses further procedures, invalidates control assumptions and never reconnects or resumes.

`PP-20260912-05` adds only the repository-local `paceprompt-video-capture` skill and its supporting scripts. Simulator capture records a selected Simulator framebuffer for a bounded duration, excludes audio, can normalise an explicitly selected landscape rotation and removes partial or rejected output. Codex workflow capture enumerates only on-screen `com.openai.codex` windows without exposing titles, binds an inspected still preview to a SHA-256 receipt and exact window geometry, requires a matching live central-content fingerprint immediately before window-only recording, omits audio and removes partial or rejected output. A separate inspector rejects audio and exports deterministic start, middle and end frames; the feature-rollout assembler accepts only individually approved silent chapters outside Git, normalises them to 1920 x 1080 H.264, limits chapter and total duration, and writes a SHA-256 manifest. The checked-in procedure uses short intent/review, Simulator-behaviour and validation chapters and requires reconstructed footage to be labelled as a review walkthrough.

`PP-20260912-03` implements only issue #60's design-aligned, physical-console Exercise UI from exact `origin/main` `eb9f01dc680f1ba2780062d4df39936e2ae997bb`, after confirming that prerequisite issues #57, #58, #59 and #81 were closed and removing issue #60's stale `status: blocked` label. A pure presentation projection consumes the frozen attempt, current reducer state and latest execution summary without performing I/O. It keeps waiting, applying, running, current-segment override, checking, paused, returning-to-plan, ending, failed and interrupted states distinct; exposes planned, effective and treadmill-reported values separately; preserves the requested, submitted, ATT-accepted, FTMS-acknowledged, observing, confirmed, stale, failed and unknown evidence stages; and emits only typed speed, inclination, return-to-plan, operator-stationary and local-End intents. The adaptive full-screen SwiftUI hierarchy covers portrait, landscape, Dynamic Type, VoiceOver order, reduced motion, minimum control sizes, full-plan review and two-step confirmations without app Start, Stop, Pause or Resume controls. Operator-stationary fallback is unavailable when fresh non-zero telemetry contradicts stationary state, and return-to-plan guidance preserves speed-before-inclination ordering.

Focused validation passed all 16 `WorkoutExercisePresentationTests` and all nine `WorkoutExerciseFlowUITests`. Independent read-only review of the first pushed head found three blocking presentation defects: joint speed-and-inclination evidence could appear confirmed before the later exact joint observation, elapsed time double-counted a completed final segment, and failed or interrupted attempts could be dismissed while motion remained possible. The first repair requires the final exact joint observation after both acknowledgements, avoids counting the completed final segment twice, and permits terminal dismissal only when the outcome is finished or motion is no longer possible. Independent review of that exact repaired pushed head confirmed the evidence and dismissal repairs but found that the elapsed-time numeric heuristic could under-count a paused segment frozen exactly at its duration. The final repair derives whether the retained current segment is already included from the execution phase and completion context, with a full-plan terminal fallback, and adds a paused-boundary regression. The final repaired executable and test diff then passed the replacement `scripts/validate_local.sh` gate: sealed resource and privacy checks, 30 scorer tests, four classification-summary tests, 82 host-evaluation tests, all 307 app unit and UI tests with zero failures or skips, production LCOV export, Debug and Release no-write binary assertions, the unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`. Evidence was retained at `/private/tmp/paceprompt-issue60-complete-final.edmiK7`. Simulator-only visual inspection and the external publishable-media package use deterministic synthetic data; the untouched original, accelerated 1080p derivative, captioned derivative, ASS captions, poster and timeline are outside Git. No redaction was required because no personal data was present. Simulator output is not physical-device, FR30z, Bluetooth, protocol-acknowledgement or machine-state evidence. No physical treadmill access or operation, Bluetooth scan or connection, FTMS Control Point `0x2AD9` write, provider call, HealthKit, Watch, dependency or signing change occurred. The protected issue #76 journal, accepted #57 profile, historical diagnostic evidence and both pre-existing stashes remain unchanged.

`PP-20260912-02` implements only issue #59's synthetic workout orchestration and incremental local-history contract from exact `origin/main` `d437052f6732b7fe391696a783acf68e08a6c7fa`, after confirming that prerequisite issues #50, #57 and #81 were closed. A local orchestration layer freezes the validated plan, exact accepted FR30z execution-profile identity, capability snapshot, session ceilings and profile values at attempt creation, then delegates every state transition to the pure issue #81 reducer. Its injected target-control transport carries one typed procedure at a time and preserves planned targets, effective targets, submitted procedures, ATT acceptance, FTMS acknowledgement and fresh observed state as distinct evidence. Deterministic clocks drive active-duration and progress checkpoints. Paused adjustments remain pending, physical resume restores speed before inclination, and local End requires stationary or operator-confirmed evidence without submitting FTMS Stop. History is created before the first mocked Request Control procedure, updated only at aggregate progress or outcome boundaries, fails closed on persistence errors, and recovers an `inProgress` attempt after restart as interrupted/unknown without reconnecting or resuming.

Focused validation passed all 12 `WorkoutExecutionOrchestratorTests`, all 26 `WorkoutExecutionReducerTests` and all 12 `WorkoutHistoryRepositoryTests`. Independent review of the first pushed head found three blocking defects: the mutable orchestrator lacked explicit serial isolation, later motion did not expire earlier operator-stationary evidence, and a history failure before transport submission could classify a definitely unforwarded target intent as delivery-unknown. The repair isolated the orchestration boundary to the main actor, expires stationary evidence from reducer-derived motion, and carries an exact definitely-not-submitted procedure ID only from an unperformed submit effect while retaining delivery-unknown for any forwarded intent. A second independent read-only review found no remaining issue in the repair diff. The complete repaired executable and test diff passed Swift format lint, `git diff --check`, complete-diff review and a safety scan before the required replacement `scripts/validate_local.sh` gate. That gate passed sealed resource and privacy checks, 30 scorer tests, four classification-summary tests, 82 host-evaluation tests with two declared optional sibling-worktree skips, all 254 app unit tests and all 28 UI tests with zero failures, production LCOV export, the unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`. Evidence was retained at `/var/folders/1b/g1kk3f852c9_5srvd67j_r9w0000gn/T/paceprompt-local-validation.83pbTL`. No production CoreBluetooth binding, Control Point write or access to `0x2AD9`, treadmill or iPhone access or operation, FTMS Start, Stop or Pause, SwiftUI, HealthKit, Watch, provider call, automatic retry or reconnection, dependency or signing change, or issue #60-or-later work was added or invoked. `PP-20260912-01` implements only the issue #58 design-aligned Preflight and waiting-for-physical-Start presentation from exact `origin/main` `41f3c74b713804168f50ee8010b5df80038d28af`, after confirming that prerequisite issues #57 and #81 were closed and that issue #58 was open without `status: blocked`. A pure presentation adapter consumes the accepted #57 profile, validated plan, conservative ceilings and current #81 reducer state to keep disconnected, preparing, unsupported, stale, locked/unknown, ready-to-request-control, requesting, ready-to-begin, waiting-for-physical-Start and failed states distinct. The full-screen waiting state instructs the operator to press Start on the physical treadmill console and labels initial speed and inclination as planned only, never submitted, acknowledged, reached or observed. Dynamic Type, VoiceOver ordering and labels, reduced-motion behaviour and DEBUG-only synthetic UI scenarios are covered; views emit intents only.

`PP-20260911-02` implements only the issue #81 pure deterministic physical-console workout-execution reducer from exact `origin/main` `ea1beefcd4126335ee455ccffe207b1be030e934`, after confirming that prerequisite issue #57 was closed and removing the stale `status: blocked` label from issue #81. The reducer consumes typed connection-epoch, exact-profile, capability, operator-readiness, telemetry, human-observation, intent-submission, ATT and FTMS acknowledgement inputs with caller-supplied monotonic time. It detects physical Start from fresh non-zero telemetry; freezes active time across delayed or stale telemetry; handles physical pause and resume; restores speed then inclination; supports independent current-segment overrides, return to plan, boundary clearing and paused adjustments; waits for physical stationary evidence before local End; and fails closed on contradictory, malformed, incomplete, rejected, timed-out, lost-control, lost-connection, background and changed-profile evidence without retry or implicit continuation. Its effects are limited to typed intent submission, local finalisation and direction to the physical console and safety key. It contains no CoreBluetooth, FTMS byte encoding, SwiftUI, persistence, timers or other I/O and defines no FTMS Start, Stop or Pause intent.

The final focused `WorkoutExecutionReducerTests` suite passed all 26 tests on the iPhone 17 Pro iOS 26.5 simulator. Complete-diff review first found and corrected pending first-segment adjustments while waiting for physical Start. Independent review of the initial pushed candidate then found that telemetry-derived End eligibility did not expire or revoke on movement, that a pending target procedure did not block End, and that retained correlation/late-evidence invariants needed explicit coverage. Replacement review found that a newer fresh zero-speed sample did not refresh the stationary evidence retained by ready-to-End and ending states. The repairs make telemetry-backed paused and ready-to-End states enter checking when evidence becomes stale, revoke End on movement, fail closed if movement or stale evidence arrives during ending, reject End while any target procedure is unresolved, restore correlation, duplicate/late and monotonic-motion coverage, and retain newer accepted stationary telemetry through local completion. The required final replacement `scripts/validate_local.sh` gate passed its sealed resource/privacy checks, host evaluation checks, all 259 app tests (236 unit and 23 UI), production coverage export, unsigned Release simulator build, static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`. No physical-device installation, Bluetooth connection, treadmill access or operation, FTMS Control Point traffic, provider call, HealthKit, Watch, dependency or signing change occurred. The protected issue #76 journal, accepted #57 profile, historical diagnostic evidence and both pre-existing stashes remain unchanged.

`PP-20260910-01` makes the single-procedure FTMS Control Point transport insensitive to the relative order of CoreBluetooth's indication and ATT write-result callbacks without weakening its evidence boundary. A well-formed response that matches the active connection epoch, procedure identifier and request opcode may be retained as provisional evidence when it arrives first, but it cannot complete the procedure or grant Control permission until the same procedure receives explicit ATT write acceptance. ATT rejection, delivery uncertainty, disconnect, malformed or mismatched bytes, a duplicate or late response, invalid monotonic time, a stale epoch or a different procedure all continue to fail closed. The 30-second indication deadline remains anchored to ATT acceptance; when a provisional response already exists, the recorded deadline is retained as evidence and no timer or second procedure is started. There is no retry, reconnection, resume, background behaviour, reducer/UI/orchestration integration or additional Control Point surface.

The adopted Bluetooth SIG Fitness Machine Service 1.0.1 and Fitness Machine Profile 1.0.1 revisions, both dated 1 October 2024, were reverified from the current official catalogue before the transport was changed. Apple's current documentation for the write-result and subscribed value-update delegate callbacks was also rechecked; it documents them independently and does not specify a relative ordering. Deterministic fake-link coverage passed all 38 codec, transport and Request Control diagnostic tests, including exact request bytes, early indication plus ATT accept/reject/unknown/disconnect, the exact 30-second deadline, malformed, mismatched, duplicate and late responses, invalid time, connection epochs and procedure identity. The complete local validation policy then passed the sealed 20-case corpus, 30 scorer tests, four summary tests, 82 host-evaluation tests with two documented optional sibling-worktree skips, all 238 production unit tests and 23 UI tests with zero failures or skips, production LCOV export without a threshold, the unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`. This is simulator and deterministic-fake evidence only, not FR30z evidence.

`PP-20260909-03` adds only a DEBUG-build, operator-supervised Request Control diagnostic for issue #51. It requires current decoded Fitness Machine Feature, speed-range and inclination-range evidence; completed passive subscription outcomes; confirmed FTMS Control Point indications; three explicit operator readiness confirmations; and a second destructive-action confirmation. A persistent one-shot gate consumes authority before forwarding and admits only the exact Request Control byte `00`; release builds compile the diagnostic path out. The diagnostic is not connected to the workout reducer, production orchestration or a general FTMS command surface, and it has no automatic retry, reconnect, resume or background behaviour. Deterministic focused coverage passed all 53 codec, transport, diagnostic-gate and presentation tests. Before installation, the complete local validation policy passed all 233 unit tests and 23 UI tests with zero failures or skips, production coverage export without a threshold, the unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all offline host and scorer checks, 23 then-current accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check` using Xcode 26.6 build 17F113 and an iPhone 17 Pro simulator running iOS 26.5. A Debug device build also succeeded with ignored local signing configuration; no signing repository change was made.

With the operator present at the identified FR30z, the deck clear, the belt stationary and immediate console and safety-key access confirmed, the app performed exactly one explicitly triggered Request Control procedure. It submitted exact request `00` once in connection epoch 1 with local procedure sequence 1. The treadmill returned well-formed success indication `80 00 01`, but CoreBluetooth delivered that indication callback before its successful ATT write callback. The reviewed transport correctly treated this as a protocol anomaly rather than success. The operator observed no movement and explicitly disconnected; CoreBluetooth confirmed the disconnect, the app was terminated, and the consumed one-shot gate prevents another write. This is a failed/unknown proof, not accepted Request Control evidence: issue #51 remains open, tracker #48 is unchanged, and target speed, target inclination, Start, Stop and Pause/Resume remain unresolved. No retry, reconnection, other FTMS Control Point command, treadmill operation, workout execution, provider request, HealthKit or Watch action occurred. The committed validation note is sanitised; the raw log containing device identifiers remains outside Git.

The same change hardens the repository-local accounting helper for a cumulative counter reset inside an exact phase. Instead of subtracting across a discontinuity, the helper now sums complete request records only after independently reconciling every monotonic cumulative epoch and requiring each reset event's new cumulative counters to equal its complete request counters field-for-field. It reports accepted in-boundary reset evidence and still fails closed on a missing or mismatched anchor. Regression coverage now comprises 24 helper tests, including exact delta and whole-session reset-spanning cases plus a corrupt-anchor rejection, and the bundled repository skill validator passed with pinned development-only PyYAML 6.0.3 in an isolated temporary environment. The final complete candidate gate then re-passed the 82 host tests with two documented optional sibling-worktree skips, sealed 20-case corpus, 30 scorer tests, four summary tests, all 233 production unit tests and 23 UI tests with zero failures or skips, production coverage export without a threshold, unsigned Release build, static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests, all 24 accounting-helper tests and `git diff --check`.

`PP-20260909-02` repairs the exact-head independent-review finding that valid FTMS range values decoded from integer fields can carry normal binary floating-point tails and were therefore rejected by the initial codec's decimal-string equality check. The codec now reverses the protocol scale and accepts only values within two ULPs of the nearest raw integer, preserving rejection of genuinely off-grid, overflowing, non-finite, out-of-range and increment-misaligned inputs. Parser-to-codec regression fixtures verify exact round trips for raw speed `1999` exposed as `19.990000000000002 km/h` and raw signed inclination `-299` exposed as `-29.900000000000002%`. The focused codec and transport suite passed all 27 tests. The complete local validation policy passed the sealed 20-case corpus, 30 scorer tests, four summary tests, 82 host-evaluation tests with two expected sibling-worktree skips, all 247 production simulator tests with zero failures or skips, the LCOV export, unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests and all 23 accounting-helper tests. No scope boundary or production integration changed.

`PP-20260909-01` implements only the bounded software delivery in issue #50. It adds an FTMS Control Point codec for Request Control, Set Target Speed, Set Target Inclination, Start and Stop; validates current decoded feature and range evidence without rounding or clamping; and decodes only exact three-byte responses for that reviewed surface. A separate single-procedure transport enables indications before any write, uses CoreBluetooth Write With Response, preserves one local procedure identifier and connection epoch, and records exact request bytes, monotonic submission time, ATT outcome, matching indication and response receipt time separately. It requires successful Request Control before any other scoped procedure, enforces the 30-second indication deadline from ATT write acceptance, and fails closed on unsupported properties, invalid values, malformed, reserved, mismatched, duplicate, out-of-order or late responses, delivery uncertainty, disconnects and invalid time. Timeout or delivery uncertainty invalidates the link and requires a separately established newer connection epoch; there is no automatic retry, reconnection, resume or background behaviour. The concrete CoreBluetooth link is deliberately not connected to the workout reducer, production UI or orchestration. The adopted Bluetooth SIG Fitness Machine Service 1.0.1 and Fitness Machine Profile 1.0.1 revisions, both dated 1 October 2024, were reverified from the current official specification catalogue and PDFs before encoding. Focused deterministic fake-link coverage passed all 26 codec and transport tests. The complete local validation policy passed the sealed 20-case corpus, 30 scorer tests, four summary tests, 82 host-evaluation tests with two expected sibling-worktree skips, all 246 production simulator tests with zero failures or skips, a non-empty 33-source LCOV export, the unsigned Release simulator build, Xcode static analysis, all 14 developer-only evaluation tests and all 23 accounting-helper tests. Validation used Xcode 26.6 build 17F113 and an iPhone 17 Pro simulator running iOS 26.5. Simulator and deterministic-fake evidence is not FR30z or physical Bluetooth evidence.

`PP-20260908-07` changes only `PacePromptUITests/PlansFlowUITests.swift` to remove two timing races exposed by post-merge run `34264076647`. The hosted attempt-1 xcresult showed `typeText("5.5")` completing before SwiftUI's formatted accessibility value settled, so the exact `5.5 km/h` assertion now waits on that value with a bounded predicate. Attempt-2 logs showed the shared scrolling helper repeatedly evaluating an already-hittable element, then treating existence alone as readiness and relying on a two-second destination wait; the helper now returns immediately only when the element is hittable and enabled, otherwise uses one bounded predicate for existence, hittability and enablement before tapping, and the preserved Review-destination assertion waits on the observable navigation state within the same ten-second interaction deadline. There are no sleeps, retries, skipped tests, assertion weakening, production changes or workflow changes. Both original paths passed three consecutive focused repetitions each (six tests, zero failures), then the complete production simulator suite passed all 220 tests (197 unit and 23 UI) with zero failures and zero skips on an iPhone 17 Pro simulator running iOS 26.5. `PP-20260908-02` delivers the bounded native SwiftUI manual-plan editor and review from designs 1f and 1g. The editor presents the canonical plan name and indoor activity, every ordered step as a scannable card, and each exact editable type, label, duration, speed and inclination value. It supports native deletion and movement plus explicit accessible move, delete and add controls, including an 18-step synthetic scroll case. Deterministic presentation mappings associate input and validation problems with the plan or exact step field while preserving unknown, unsupported, malformed and out-of-range capability states; the grouped guidance rejects invalid values without clamping or repairing them and blocks review until the draft changes. `PP-20260907-18` delivers the bounded software-only issue #37 execution-summary storage slice. The versioned `WorkoutExecutionSummary` preserves a complete immutable `WorkoutPlan` snapshot, optional source-plan identity, attempt and update times, one of the five accepted outcomes, explicit measured-or-unavailable duration and distance, progress, and physical-stop confirmation. Reason codes remain non-empty opaque stable values; the repository does not author or interpret execution outcomes. The separate Foundation-only `workout-history.json` repository preserves record order, appends new attempt identifiers and replaces an existing attempt in place only when its immutable fields match and its update time does not move backwards. It distinguishes empty, protected-data unavailable, read failure, corrupt, partial-write, stale-staging, unsupported store, unsupported summary and unsupported nested-plan states; closed stored JSON shapes and structural corruption block mutation. Complete protection, backup exclusion, staged validation, synchronisation and atomic replacement are required, with the previous canonical bytes preserved across every injected write-stage failure. Nothing is wired to History presentation or workout execution. Focused history coverage passed 12 tests. The complete production simulator suite passed 145 tests, including 10 UI tests, with no failures or skips. The Release simulator build, static analysis, Xcode project validation, production-bundle exclusion check, 23 accounting-helper tests, `git diff --check` and full scope review also passed. Validation used an iPhone 17 Pro simulator running iOS 26.5. The initial sandboxed focused-test attempt could not access CoreSimulator and is not validation evidence; the subsequent focused and complete runs used the authorised simulator service. Simulator tests verify requested protection operations and failure handling, not real iPhone Data Protection behaviour; physical-device validation remains unperformed and separately authorised.

`PP-20260907-17` delivers the bounded software-only issue #35 export flow. A user can select one or more decoded supported saved plans, review the exact filename, category, count and every included JSON field path, then invoke a separate system Share action. `PP-20260907-14` covers the bounded software-only issue #31 implementation of confirmed single-plan deletion in the Plans tab. The repository protocol now exposes the existing exact-identifier deletion operation, each saved-plan row offers a deliberate non-full-swipe destructive action, and the named permanent-deletion alert binds confirmation to the selected record identity. Cancellation performs no mutation.
